500 Easy DSA Coding Practice Questions
Structured beginner-level problem bank for coding-platform ingestion. Each problem includes statement,
constraints, I/O format, examples, visible tests, and hidden-test design guidance.
1. Find the maximum element in an array
Category: Arrays Difficulty: Easy Tags: arrays, beginner
Problem Statement: Solve the following task: find the maximum element in an array. Read the input, perform the required operation,
and print the result. Your solution should handle all valid inputs within the stated constraints.
Constraints: 1 ≤ N ≤ 105 unless otherwise stated; values fit in a signed 32-bit integer; input contains valid data.
Input Format: The first line contains N. The next line contains N space-separated values, or the string/parameters required by the
problem.
Output Format: Print the required answer on one line. Do not print extra explanatory text.
Example: Input: 5 | 1 2 3 4 5 Output: 5 (illustrative; adapt according to the exact task).
Visible Test Cases: (1) Smallest valid input. (2) Typical mixed input. (3) Already processed or uniform input. (4) Boundary-sized
values.
Hidden Test Cases: Include minimum N, maximum N, duplicates, negative values where valid, all-equal values, sorted and
reverse-sorted data, empty-result scenarios where valid, and overflow-sensitive sums/products.
Judge Notes: Compare normalized output exactly; reject extra text. Add category-specific edge cases and randomized tests before
publishing.
